1026. EDWARD MERRYWEATHER was indicated for stealing 1 sixpence, the monies of Flavius Josephus Hudson, his master.
FLAVIUS JOSEPHUS HUDSON . I ama grocer, and live in York-place, Old Kent-road—the prisoner was my shopman for six montha—the first two months he had 3 s. a week, and then 3 s. 6 d.—he had his board—in consequence of suspicion, I opened his box with a key, on Friday, the 28th of March, and on that evening I left in my till, as usual, two sixpences, and 1 s. worth of coppers—next morning I came down, about eight o'clock—I
then found in the till 3 s. 6 d. in silver, and 9 1/2 d. in coppers—directly after breakfast I said to the prisoner, "You must have money in your pocket, which does not belong to you; I can bring parties to prove they have laid out 3 s. this morning, and you have only 2 s. 3 1/2 d. to show for it" (that was besides the 2 s. I had left in the till the night previous)—I said, "I must see what you have in your pocket"—he said, "You are quite welcome"—he took out of his pocket one shilling, one sixpence, and 2 3/4 d., and laid it on the counter—he said, "You see I have no marked money here; when you mark money again, take care to mark it smaller, for I saw the money was marked yesterday"—I examined the money he laid down, and observed the sixpence was marked;—I said, "Here, then, is a sixpence marked smaller, how did you come by that?"—he made no answer—I then said, "I must see in your box"—he said, "You shall, before I go"—I said, "No, I must see at once"—he said, "You shall, if you fetch a policeman"—I fetched one, and we went up stairs together—the prisoner opened his box, and we found in it a tin box, containing 17 s. in silver—he turned out the silver, and put the box down again—I took it up, and opened an interior box, and turned out of it eleven sovereigns—I said, "How did you come by all this money? it cannot be yours, from the low wages you have had here, and what you had prior to your coming, being out of place, this money can't belong to you"—he said, "No, part of it belongs to my brother, and part is mine"—I said, "You know very well this is not your money; this belongs to me; you might have a little money belonging to you; if you like to take the silver and copper, you may go away"—being a father, I did not wish to put the law in full force against him—he said, "No," he should not do that, and I gave him in charge—this is the marked sixpence—here is a small scratch on the nose, which my wife made, but not in my presence—I only saw it when I took it out of the till.
Cross-examined by MR. ROBINSON. Q. When you made him this liberal offer, he said, "I shall not give it up, I will go to the station? A. Yes—I opened his box the day before with a key from a bunch of keys we have in our possession'—the first key I tried fitted the box—I had never looked into his box before—my wife looked in after I had named the circumstance to her—I looked in on the Friday that we marked the money, and I looked in again the next morning, and 3 s. 6 d. more had been added from about the middle of Friday—I had paid him his week's wages on Saturday, the 22nd—when I looked into his box on the Friday I saw coppers amounting to nearly 2 s., and I saw him with a respectable suit of clothes on—that gave me suspicion, which was on my mind for a considerable time—there was a deficiency of money on the Thursday, when my wife said she had noticed him serve a great many customers—no other persons go into the shop—no person goes behind the counter but my own children—I found only one marked sixpence on the prisoner—there were two marked shillings in the till that morning, and one shilling and a sixpence not marked.
COURT. Q. You only put two sixpences and a shilling's worth of copper into the till? A. Yes, I did, but there had been two marked shillings passed by customers that morning—I found the two marked shillings in the till, and only one marked sixpence—when the prisoner told me, when I marked money again to mark it smaller, I had not said anything to him about marked money.
MR. ROBINSON. Q. It was a tin box of the prisoner's in which this money was kept? A. Yes, the gold was in another box inside the tin box—I indicted him for stealing the eleven sovereigns—I have been told the Grand Jury have thrown out that bill—I had frequently missed money—I do not apprehend it was taken in sovereigns.
MARY ANN HUDSON . I am the prosecutor's wife. I saw the eleven sovereigns and 13 s. 6 d. in the prisoner's box on the Friday afternoon, and that evening I marked two shillings and two sixpences—I gave the two shillings to Mr. Fairing ton, and the two sixpences to Mrs. Pritchard, to lay out at our shop—on this sixpence produced here is a scratch at the nose, and a piece cut out at the side, in a line with it—I know the mark, and the date of the sixpence was taken as well—I can speak to this as my own mark—I never marked any other sixpences in my life.
WILLIAM FARRINOTON . I am a baker, and live in Windsor-place, Old Kent-road. My wife showed me two marked shillings—I sent the servant girl with them to the prosecutor's shop for some articles—I took a memorandum of the date and reign of the two shillings—I received two marked sixpences from Mrs. Pritchard's sister—I put down the date and reign of them—they were marked on the top of the nose and on the edge—I sent the boy out with both the sixpences—this sixpence is one of them—it is of the year 1840—it is one that Mrs. Pritchard's sister gave me, and one I sent by the boy.
Joseph Biggs. I am employed occasionally by Mr. Farrington to go on errands. On that Saturday morning, about twenty minutes to eight o'clock, he gave me two sixpences, to go to Mr. Hudson's shop, to fetch 2oz. of tea and 1 lb. of sugar—they came to 1 s.—I paid the two sixpences for them that I had from Mr. Farrington—I did not notice them.
BENJAMIN —( police-constable M 107.) I searched the prisoner's box with Mr. Hudson—I found in it eleven sovereigns, fifteen shillings, four sixpences, and a lot of coppers—I heard Mr. Hudson say to the prisoner, "There is no doubt the sovereigns belong to me; if you will give me the sovereigns, you may take the silver, and go about your business"—the prisoner said part of the money belonged to his brother—this is the box—it has been a lucifer-box, I believe—this is the marked sixpence I had of Mr. Hudson.
MR. ROBINSON called
MRS. MERRYWEATHER. I am the prisoner's mother. I know he had money in his possession about last June—I saw it—there were six sovereigns and some silver—he was of a remarkably saving turn—I know of his having a suit of clothes nearly two years—he got them at his first place—they were left by his master for mourning.
CATHARINE EVANS . I live in Waterloo-street, Walworth. I have known the prisoner three years—I saw some money in his box, where he placed his clothes, three or four months before he went to Mr. Hudson's—he had six sovereigns, besides some silver, which I did not see counted he had a black coat, black trowsera, and a blue satin waistcoat.
COURT to MR. HUDSON. Q. What were the clothes the prisoner had? A. He had a blue satin waistcoat for one thing—I think his coat was black, and he had a pair of Wellington boots, which appeared new—I had missed money frequently—I reckoned up the wages I had paid him, and they were about 4 l. 7 s.—he has a brother in some employ—I missed 8 1/2 d. in amount that morning—I knew it was the intention to lay out the whole
of the 2 s. at my shop, and I ascertained that the whole of it was laid out—Mr. Farrington has the articles put down that were bought, but the woman who bought them is not here—she was at the office, but not bound over—I attend very closely to the business of my shop, but I was not there when the 2 s. were laid out—it is my custom to give the prisoner the key in a morning, and he comes down and opens the shop while I am dressing.
(The prisoner received a good character.)
GUILTY . Aged 20.— Confined Three Months.

1031. JAMES HATCHER was indicted for stealing 2 coats, value 3 l., the goods of John Mumford; and that he had been before convicted of felony; to which he pleaded
GUILTY . Aged 19.— Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18450407-1032]
1032. WILLIAM WRIGHT was indicted for stealing 1 1/2 lb. weight of tripe, value 8 d.; 1/2 lb. weight of sausages, 3 d.; 1 loaf of bread, 3 d.; and 1 handkerchief, 7 d.; the goods of William Browning, from the person of Mary Ann Browning; and that he had been before convicted of felony.
MARY ANN BROWNING . I am the wife of William Browning, of King William-street. On the evening of the 20th of March I was in Tooley-street, carrying a bundle, containing the tripe, the bread, and other things mentioned—I was pushed down in the street, and my bundle taken from me—I am quite sure I was pushed down, and with violence—I fell down, and my bundle was snatched from me—the prisoner is the man who pushed me down, and who took my bundle—I am sure of that—I saw him, and followed him—I met with the officer—this is my handkerchief—I can swear to it—it was round the bundle, and a loaf was found, which resembled the loaf I lost.
HENRY HUNT ( police-constable M 82.) Between six and seven o'clock that evening I was on duty in Tooley-street—I received information from Mrs. Browning—I went and met the prisoner at the bottom of Ball-court, with a loaf in one hand, and a handkerchief in the other—I said, "What is this?"—he said, "Some bread, and this is my handkerchief"—I did not
take him then, but went and spoke to the prosecutrix—I then went back to the prisoner—he still had the loaf in his hand—he threw it down—this handkerchief I found in his pocket.
Prisoner. Q. Did I not say I picked them up? A. No.
Prisoner. I picked them up in the court; the officer came, and I told him what I had got; he came back in five minutes, and took me; I made no resistance; the prosecutrix said she thought I was the person.
MARY ANN BROWNING re-examined. I am quite certain he is the person—I never thought anything about it.
THOMAS WEST ( police-constable M 249.) I produce a certificate of the prisoner's former conviction, which I got at the Surrey Session—( read—Convicted on the 5 th of May, 1844, and confined six months.)
GUILTY . Aged 19.— Transported for Ten Years.
[trialAccount t18450407-1032a]
1032. EDWARD OWEN was indicted for embezzling 2 l. 15 s., of John Johnson Kirby, his master, to which he pleaded
GUILTY . Aged 45.— Confined Nine Months.
[trialAccount t18450407-1033]
1033. HANNAH LEARY was indicted for stealing 3 lbs. weight of beef, value 1 s. 3 d., the property of Joseph Smith; and that she had been before convicted of felony.
JOSEPH SMITH . I am a butcher, and live in the Lower Marsh, Lambeth. On the 15th of March, about half-past ten o'clock at night, I was outside my door, and saw the prisoner come and take a piece of salt beef out of the salt beef tray—she walked off with it—I followed, and asked her to give it me—she would not, but dropped it—it was mine.
Prisoner. Q. Did you not take another woman as well as me? A. Yes, I thought she was connected with her, but the prisoner took the beef.
Prisoner's Defence. I am quite innocent.
VALENTINE TITE ( police-constable L 45.) I produce a certificate of the prisoner's conviction, which I got from the Sessions House, Newington—( read—Convicted on the 2 nd of December, 1844, andconfined three months)—she is the person.
GUILTY . Aged 24.— Confined Six Months.
ADJOURNED TO MONDAY THE 12 TH OF MAY.
